truncated output (original token count: 11146)\\nTotal output lines: 824\\n\\n# Backend Coding Standard\\r\\n\\r\\n> **Project:** Workflow Automation / FlowPilot  \\r\\n> **Stack:** NestJS, TypeScript, PostgreSQL, Prisma, Redis, BullMQ  \\r\\n> **Architecture:** Modular Monolith + Clean Architecture + CQRS  \\r\\n> **Runtime concerns:** Workflow execution, connectors, background jobs, SSE\\r\\n\\r\\nThis document defines backend conventions for developers and coding agents. It is intended to preserve the current architecture while making module boundaries, error handling, testing, and operational behavior explicit.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Core principles\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Business rules are explicit and testable.**\\r\\n2. **Dependencies point inward.** Domain and application code must not depend on infrastructure or transport frameworks.\\r\\n3. **Modules own their data and behavior.** Cross-module collaboration happens through deliberate public contracts.\\r\\n4. **Use CQRS where it clarifies intent**, not as ceremony for every simple operation.\\r\\n5. **Reliability is part of correctness.** Background jobs, workflow execution, and connector calls must account for retries, duplicate delivery, timeouts, and partial failure.\\r\\n6. **Prefer the simplest design that preserves required boundaries.**\\r\\n7. **Refactor safely.** Preserve behavior, add characterization/regression tests, and avoid unrelated rewrites.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Naming conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n| Item | Convention | Example |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| File | kebab-case + role suffix | `start-execution.handler.ts` |\\r\\n| Class | PascalCase | `StartExecutionHandler` |\\r\\n| Interface / port | PascalCase, descriptive name | `ExecutionRepository` |\\r\\n| Plugin contract | Existing `I` prefix may be retained consistently | `INodeDefinition` |\\r\\n| Enum | PascalCase; values follow domain convention | `ExecutionStatus.RUNNING` |\\r\\n| Variable/function | camelCase | `workflowId`, `startExecution()` |\\r\\n| Constant | UPPER_SNAKE_CASE for true constants | `MAX_RETRY_COUNT` |\\r\\n| DTO | PascalCase + `Dto` | `CreateWorkflowDto` |\\r\\n| Command/query/event | PascalCase + role suffix | `StartExecutionCommand` |\\r\\n| Module directory | kebab-case, singular concept | `execution/`, `connector/` |\\r\\n| Database table | Follow Prisma schema convention consistently | `workflow_executions` |\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not introduce a naming convention that conflicts with the existing codebase without a migration plan. Names should express domain intent rather than implementation detail.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Architecture and dependency rules\\r\\n\\r\\nThe intended dependency direction is:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\n                 ┌──────────────────────┐\\r\\n                 │     Presentation     │\\r\\n                 │ Controllers, DTOs    │\\r\\n                 └──────────┬───────────┘\\r\\n                            v\\r\\n                 ┌──────────────────────┐\\r\\n                 │     Application      │\\r\\n                 │ Use cases, handlers  │\\r\\n                 │ Ports, orchestration │\\r\\n                 └──────────┬───────────┘\\r\\n                            v\\r\\n                 ┌──────────────────────┐\\r\\n                 │        Domain        │\\r\\n                 │ Rules, entities, VOs │\\r\\n                 └──────────────────────┘\\r\\n\\r\\nInfrastructure implements ports defined inward:\\r\\nInfrastructure ───────> Application / Domain contracts\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3.1 Domain\\r\\n\\r\\n- Contains domain entities, value objects, domain services, domain events, and domain-level contracts.\\r\\n- Must not import NestJS, Prisma, BullMQ, HTTP clients, database drivers, or presentation DTOs.\\r\\n- Must not depend on infrastructure implementations.\\r\\n- Owns business invariants and state transitions.\\r\\n- Prefer identity references (IDs) across module boundaries instead of holding another module's entity object.\\r\\n- Keep domain objects independent of persistence shape.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3.2 Application\\r\\n\\r\\n- Contains use cases, command/query handlers, orchestration services, application ports, and transaction boundaries.\\r\\n- May use `@nestjs/cqrs` as an application delivery mechanism, but business logic must not depend on Nest HTTP concepts.\\r\\n- Must not import Prisma clients or concrete infrastructure adapters.\\r\\n- Coordinates domain objects and ports; it should not become a second domain layer full of business rules.\\r\\n- Application errors should describe use-case outcomes, not HTTP status codes.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3.3 Infrastructure\\r\\n\\r\\n- Implements repository and service ports.\\r\\n- Owns Prisma, Redis, BullMQ, external HTTP clients, filesystem access, and framework-specific adapters.\\r\\n- Converts infrastructure failures into stable application-facing errors where the use case needs to distinguish them.\\r\\n- Must not leak raw database or provider-specific errors through public API contracts.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3.4 Presentation\\r\\n\\r\\n- Owns HTTP controllers, request/response DTOs, guards, interceptors, pipes, and transport mapping.\\r\\n- Validates request shape and authentication/authorization context.\\r\\n- Dispatches use cases through application interfaces or CQRS buses.\\r\\n- Maps application outcomes to HTTP responses.\\r\\n- Must not contain business workflows or direct database access.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3.5 Module boundaries\\r\\n\\r\\n- A module exposes a small, intentional public API through its Nest module exports or public entry point.\\r\\n- Do not import another module's private implementation files.\\r\\n- Do not access another module's repository or database tables directly as a shortcut.\\r\\n- Prefer a public application port, facade, command, query, or domain event for cross-module collaboration.\\r\\n- Avoid circular module dependencies. Do not use `forwardRef()` as the default solution; first inspect ownership and dependency direction.\\r\\n- Cross-module domain references should generally use IDs and explicit contracts.\\r\\n- Keep shared-kernel code small. Do not move feature-specific logic into shared code merely to avoid an import.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Module structure\\r\\n\\r\\nA feature module may use the following structure where its complexity justifies it:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nexecution/\\r\\n├── domain/\\r\\n│   ├── entities/\\r\\n│   ├── value-objects/\\r\\n│   ├── events/\\r\\n│   └── repositories/\\r\\n├── application/\\r\\n│   ├── commands/\\r\\n│   ├── queries/\\r\\n│   ├── services/\\r\\n│   └── ports/\\r\\n├── infrastructure/\\r\\n│   ├── persistence/\\r\\n│   ├── queue/\\r\\n│   └── adapters/\\r\\n├── presentation/\\r\\n│   ├── controllers/\\r\\n│   └── dto/\\r\\n├── execution.module.ts\\r\\n└── index.ts\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not create empty layers or folders for a small module. The structure should reflect actual responsibilities.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Dependency injection\\r\\n\\r\\n- Prefer constructor injection.\\r\\n- Depend on application/domain contracts rather than concrete infrastructure classes when crossing architectural boundaries.\\r\\n- TypeScript interfaces are erased at runtime and cannot be used directly as Nest provider tokens. Use a symbol token or abstract class.\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts\\r\\n// execution.tokens.ts\\r\\nexport const EXECUTION_REPOSITORY = Symbol(\\\"EXECUTION_REPOSITORY\\\");\\r\\n\\r\\n// execution.module.ts\\r\\n{\\r\\n  provide: EXECUTION_REPOSITORY,\\r\\n  useClass: PrismaExecutionRepository,\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\n// handler.ts\\r\\nconstructor(\\r\\n  @Inject(EXECUTION_REPOSITORY)\\r\\n  private readonly repository: ExecutionRepository,\\r\\n) {}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep provider registration close to the module that owns the implementation.\\r\\n- Export only providers required by other modules.\\r\\n- Do not instantiate injectable services manually with `new` in application code.\\r\\n- Avoid service locator patterns and property injection.\\r\\n- Do not add a DI token for every internal class when ordinary constructor composition is sufficient.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. CQRS and use-case conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- Use commands for operations that change state and queries for read operations.\\r\\n- A command/query handler should have one clear use-case responsibility.\\r\\n- Keep handlers focused on orchestration: load required data, invoke domain behavior, coordinate ports, and return an application result.\\r\\n- Put invariants and business decisions in domain entities/services or a clearly named policy, not in controllers or generic utility functions.\\r\\n- Keep commands and queries immutable (`readonly` fields).\\r\\n- Do not return persistence models directly from public API endpoints.\\r\\n- Map results to response DTOs at the presentation boundary or through an explicit mapper.\\r\\n- Use events for meaningful facts that have occurred, not as an indirect replacement for ordinary method calls.\\r\\n- Event and queue consumers must be idempotent where duplicate delivery is possible.\\r\\n- Avoid creating a command/handler pair for trivial internal operations when it adds ceremony without improving separation.\\r\\n\\r\\nSuggested file convention:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nstart-execution/\\r\\n├── start-execution.command.ts\\r\\n├── start-execution.handler.ts\\r\\n└── start-execution.result.ts\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nKeeping command and handler together is acceptable for small use cases; separate them when the handler or result grows. Follow one convention consistently within a module.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. Error handling\\r\\n\\r\\nUse errors that communicate the layer and meaning of a failure.\\r\\n\\r\\n- Domain errors represent violated business invariants.\\r\\n- Application errors represent use-case outcomes, such as workflow not found, invalid state transition, or connector unavailable.\\r\\n- Infrastructure errors represent technical failures and should be translated at the adapter boundary when the application needs a stable abstraction.\\r\\n- HTTP status mapping belongs in presentation (exception filters or controller boundary).\\r\\n- Do not throw `BadRequestException`, `NotFoundException`, or other Nest HTTP exceptions from domain or application handlers.\\r\\n- Do not expose stack traces, SQL details, provider responses, secrets, or internal implementation details to API clients.\\r\\n- Do not catch an error merely to rethrow the same error without adding context or changing behavior.\\r\\n- Never use empty catch blocks.\\r\\n- Preserve the original cause when wrapping errors where supported.\\r\\n\\r\\nExample:\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts\\r\\n// Application layer\\r\\nthrow new WorkflowNotFoundError(workflowId);\\r\\n\\r\\n// Presentation boundary / exception filter\\r\\n// WorkflowNotFoundError -> HTTP 404\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nUse centralized exception mapping for consistent API responses. Controllers should not each implement their own error translation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 8. Validation and API contracts\\r\\n\\r\\n- Validate external input at the presentation boundary using DTOs and the configured validation pipe.\\r\\n- Configure whitelist behavior and reject unexpected properties where appropriate.\\r\\n- Treat webhook payloads, connector responses, queue messages, and persisted JSON as untrusted input.\\r\\n- Validate domain invariants in the domain/application layer even if the frontend already validates them.\\r\\n- Do not use DTO validation as a replacement for authorization or business validation.\\r\\n- Keep request/response contracts stable and version changes deliberately.\\r\\n- Avoid returning ORM entities or internal database fields directly.\\r\\n- Use explicit pagination, sorting, and filtering contracts; enforce reasonable limits.\\r\\n- Normalize errors into the project's documented API error shape.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 9. Persistence and transactions\\r\\n\\r\\n- Prisma and database-specific code belong in infrastructure.\\r\\n- Repositories should expose domain/application-oriented operations, not simply mirror every Prisma method.\\r\\n- Keep transaction boundaries at the use-case/application level when one operation spans multiple repositories or writes.\\r\\n- Do not open nested transactions accidentally.\\r\\n- Make transaction scope as short as practical; do not hold a database transaction open while waiting for an external API or long-running job.\\r\\n- Enforce important uniqueness and consistency rules in the database as well as application logic.\\r\\n- Use migrations for schema changes; do not rely on manual production database edits.\\r\\n- Avoid N+1 queries and unbounded reads. Select only required fields and paginate large result sets.\\r\\n- Treat JSON columns as versioned contracts when their structure is persisted over time.\\r\\n- Use optimistic concurrency or explicit locking when concurrent updates could violate invariants.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 10. Background jobs and workflow execution\\r\\n\\r\\nWorkflow execution and connector calls are distributed operations, even when deployed as a modular monolith.\\r\\n\\r\\n- Assume a job may be delivered more than once.\\r\\n- Design job handlers and event consumers to be idempotent.\\r\\n- Define retryable versus non-retryable failures. Do not retry validation, authorization, or permanent business failures indefinitely.\\r\\n- Use bounded retries with backoff and jitter where appropriate.\\r\\n- Set explicit timeouts for external calls and jobs.\\r\\n- Define job identity, correlation IDs, and execution/node identifiers consistently.\\r\\n- Persist state transitions deliberately; do not rely only on in-memory state.\\r\\n- Ensure terminal execution states cannot be overwritten by late duplicate events.\\r\\n- Handle cancellation, timeout, retry, and partial failure as explicit state transitions.\\r\\n- Avoid holding database transactions open across queue publishing or external network calls.\\r\\n- Consider the dual-write problem when persisting state and publishing a job/event. Use an outbox or another documented reliability strategy when loss would violate correctness.\\r\\n- Do not assume queue ordering or exactly-once delivery unless the infrastructure explicitly guarantees it.\\r\\n- Keep workflow definitions immutable after publication; execution should reference a stable version.\\r\\n- Keep the workflow engine independent from transport and queue implementation details.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 11. Connector and external integration safety\\r\\n\\r\\n- Treat connector configuration, credentials, webhook payloads, and provider responses as untrusted.\\r\\n- Never log access tokens, refresh tokens, API keys, passwords, or raw credential-bearing payloads.\\r\\n- Encrypt credentials at rest and restrict decryption to the smallest required boundary.\\r\\n- Validate OAuth `state`, redirect URI, scopes, and callback ownership.\\r\\n- Define token refresh concurrency behavior to prevent refresh races.\\r\\n- Apply timeouts, bounded retries, and provider-specific rate-limit handling.\\r\\n- Prevent SSRF in user-configurable HTTP connectors: restrict schemes, validate resolved addresses, block loopback/private/link-local/metadata ranges as appropriate, and revalidate redirects.\\r\\n- Protect webhook endpoints against replay and unauthorized invocation using signatures, timestamps, secrets, or provider-supported verification.\\r\\n- Do not trust a connector's declared schema as proof that its runtime output is valid.\\r\\n- Sanitize provider error messages before returning them to users.\\r\\n- Keep provider-specific behavior inside connector adapters, not generic workflow orchestration.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 12. Authentication and authorization\\r\\n\\r\\n- Authentication establishes identity; authorization decides whether that identity may perform an action on a resource.\\r\\n- Enforce authorization on every protected backend operation, not only in controllers or the frontend.\\r\\n- Check resource ownership and tenant/workspace scope explicitly.\\r\\n- Apply least privilege to service credentials and internal module access.\\r\\n- Avoid trusting user IDs, roles, or tenant IDs supplied in request bodies when they should come from verified identity context.\\r\\n- Record security-sensitive administrative actions in an audit trail.\\r\\n- Do not expose whether a protected resource exists when that would leak information.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 13. Logging and observability\\r\\n\\r\\n- Use the project's approved structured logger and consistent log levels.\\r\\n- Include useful correlation context where available: `requestId`, `workflowId`, `executionId`, `nodeId`, and job ID.\\r\\n- Never log secrets, tokens, passwords, credential values, or sensitive personal data.\\r\\n- Log state transitions and operational failures at the layer that has sufficient context.\\r\\n- Avoid logging the same exception at every layer.\\r\\n- Do not use `console.log` in production code.\\r\\n- Metrics and traces should help answer operational questions: execution success/failure, queue latency, node duration, retry count, connector latency, and error category.\\r\\n- Avoid high-cardinality metric labels such as raw user IDs, workflow IDs, or arbitrary URLs.\\r\\n- Ensure health checks distinguish process liveness from dependency readiness.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 14. Configuration and secrets\\r\\n\\r\\n- Read environment configuration through a centralized, validated configuration mechanism.\\r\\n- Fail fast on missing required configuration.\\r\\n- Do not read `process.env` throughout business logic.\\r\\n- Never commit `.env` files containing real credentials.\\r\\n- Provide safe example configuration with placeholder values.\\r\\n- Keep environment-specific configuration outside source code.\\r\\n- Rotate secrets through the deployment/secret-management process; do not hardcode fallback production secrets.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 15. TypeScript and code quality\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep `strict` TypeScript settings enabled and improve legacy code incrementally rather than weakening the compiler.\\r\\n- Do not introduce `any`. Use `unknown` with type guards for untrusted or uncertain values.\\r\\n- A…1960 tokens truncated…ients and libraries\\r\\n├── routes/                    # Route composition, if not owned by app/\\r\\n├── types/                     # Types genuinely shared across features\\r\\n└── utils/                     # Small, pure, generic helpers\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n### Boundary rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- A feature owns its pages, components, hooks, API functions, services, stores, and types.\\r\\n- A feature must not import another feature's internal file through a deep relative path.\\r\\n- Cross-feature imports should use the exporting feature's public entry point (`features/<name>/index.ts`) or a deliberately shared module.\\r\\n- Do not move code into `components/`, `hooks/`, `types/`, or `utils/` merely because it is used twice. Promote it only when it is genuinely generic and has stable ownership.\\r\\n- Avoid circular dependencies between features.\\r\\n- Keep `app/` focused on composition and application bootstrap; do not place feature business rules there.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Naming and file conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n| Item | Convention | Example |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| React component | PascalCase | `WorkflowCard.tsx` |\\r\\n| Component test | Component name + `.test` | `WorkflowCard.test.tsx` |\\r\\n| Hook | `use` + PascalCase | `useWorkflowMutations.ts` |\\r\\n| Service | kebab-case + `.service` | `workflow.service.ts` |\\r\\n| API module | kebab-case + `.api` | `workflow.api.ts` |\\r\\n| Store | kebab-case + `.store` | `designer.store.ts` |\\r\\n| Utility | descriptive kebab-case | `parse-cron.ts` |\\r\\n| Type module | domain name | `workflow.types.ts` |\\r\\n| Constants | descriptive kebab-case | `workflow.constants.ts` |\\r\\n\\r\\nUse names that describe intent. Avoid vague names such as `data.ts`, `helpers.ts`, `common.ts`, or `manager.ts` unless their scope is genuinely clear.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. TypeScript rules\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep TypeScript strict mode enabled for application code. Do not weaken compiler settings to make a change pass.\\r\\n- Do not introduce `any`. Use a concrete type, a generic, or `unknown` followed by runtime validation/narrowing.\\r\\n- Do not use `as` casts to silence a type error when the value can be validated or modeled correctly.\\r\\n- Avoid non-null assertions (`!`). If a value is required, establish that invariant explicitly.\\r\\n- Prefer `type` for unions, intersections, mapped types, and aliases; use `interface` when declaration merging or extension of an object contract is useful. Be consistent within a domain.\\r\\n- Use `readonly` for values that should not be mutated.\\r\\n- Model API responses and external data as untrusted input. Validate at runtime when correctness or security depends on the shape.\\r\\n- Avoid duplicating backend contracts manually when a generated or shared contract is available and maintained.\\r\\n- Export types intentionally; do not create a global type dumping ground.\\r\\n\\r\\n```ts\\r\\ntype ExecutionStatus = \\\"PENDING\\\" | \\\"RUNNING\\\" | \\\"SUCCEEDED\\\" | \\\"FAILED\\\";\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface WorkflowSummary {\\r\\n  id: string;\\r\\n  name: string;\\r\\n  status: ExecutionStatus;\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. React component design\\r\\n\\r\\n- Use function components.\\r\\n- Keep components focused on rendering and coordinating UI interactions.\\r\\n- Extract a child component when it has an independent responsibility, meaningful reuse, or makes the parent substantially easier to understand.\\r\\n- Keep complex state transitions and reusable behavior in hooks or feature services.\\r\\n- Do not create a hook for trivial one-line logic used only once.\\r\\n- Avoid deeply nested conditional JSX. Use early returns or small named components when they improve readability.\\r\\n- Use stable keys for rendered collections. Never use array indexes as keys when items can be inserted, removed, or reordered.\\r\\n- Keep effects for synchronization with external systems. Do not use effects to derive values that can be computed during render.\\r\\n- Do not suppress React hook dependency warnings without documenting and understanding the reason.\\r\\n- Do not add `React.memo`, `useMemo`, or `useCallback` by default. Use them when profiling, expensive computation, referential stability requirements, or a known rendering issue justifies them.\\r\\n- Do not define components inside other components unless there is a clear reason and remount behavior is understood.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Component size\\r\\n\\r\\nLine counts are review signals, not hard limits. A 250-line cohesive component may be clearer than several artificial wrappers. Split a file when it has multiple responsibilities, difficult-to-follow state transitions, or independent testing needs.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. State management\\r\\n\\r\\nChoose state ownership deliberately:\\r\\n\\r\\n| State | Preferred owner | Examples |\\r\\n|---|---|---|\\r\\n| State used by one component | `useState` / `useReducer` | Dialog open, input draft |\\r\\n| Server state | TanStack Query | Workflows, executions, connectors |\\r\\n| Shared client/UI state | Zustand | Selected node, canvas state, undo/redo, sidebar |\\r\\n| URL/shareable navigation state | Router/search params | Filters, selected tab, page |\\r\\n\\r\\nRules:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Do not copy API responses into Zustand when TanStack Query can own the server state.\\r\\n- Do not use TanStack Query as a general-purpose UI state store.\\r\\n- Avoid duplicating the same state in multiple stores.\\r\\n- Keep Zustand stores focused. Prefer selectors to subscribing components to the entire store.\\r\\n- Reset feature state when its lifecycle ends if stale state could affect another workflow or user session.\\r\\n- Keep derived values derived; do not store them unless there is a measured reason.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. Data access and API layering\\r\\n\\r\\nUse a simple, consistent flow:\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nPage / Component\\r\\n       |\\r\\n       v\\r\\nFeature hook (TanStack Query)\\r\\n       |\\r\\n       v\\r\\nFeature service (only when orchestration/mapping is needed)\\r\\n       |\\r\\n       v\\r\\nAPI module / shared API client\\r\\n       |\\r\\n       v\\r\\nBackend\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nRules:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Components must not construct raw HTTP requests.\\r\\n- Query and mutation hooks should own cache keys, invalidation, optimistic updates, and request lifecycle.\\r\\n- API modules own transport details: URL, HTTP method, headers, serialization, and response parsing.\\r\\n- A service is appropriate when it coordinates multiple API calls, applies feature-level mapping, or encapsulates reusable feature behavior. Do not add a service that only forwards one function without adding value.\\r\\n- Centralize authentication, base URL, common headers, and transport-level error handling in the configured API client.\\r\\n- Keep query keys centralized or consistently factory-based within each feature.\\r\\n- Invalidate the smallest correct query scope after mutations.\\r\\n- Use optimistic updates only when rollback and reconciliation behavior are defined.\\r\\n- Do not swallow errors or convert every failure into a generic success-shaped response.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 8. Forms and validation\\r\\n\\r\\n- Use React Hook Form for forms with meaningful state, validation, or dynamic fields.\\r\\n- Use Zod for schemas where runtime validation is needed; infer TypeScript types from schemas where practical.\\r\\n- Keep field-level validation and user-facing messages close to the form.\\r\\n- Validate on the client for usability, but treat backend validation as authoritative.\\r\\n- Disable duplicate submission while a mutation is pending when duplicate requests would be harmful.\\r\\n- Preserve user input when a recoverable submission error occurs.\\r\\n- Ensure labels, descriptions, errors, and controls are programmatically associated.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 9. UI, styling, and accessibility\\r\\n\\r\\n- Prefer existing shadcn/ui components and shared project components before building a new primitive.\\r\\n- shadcn/ui is a preferred component foundation, not a requirement to wrap every HTML element. Semantic native HTML is appropriate.\\r\\n- Extend or compose existing components instead of copying their implementation.\\r\\n- Use Tailwind utilities for ordinary layout and styling. Add CSS modules or standalone CSS when styles are complex, reusable, or clearer outside class strings.\\r\\n- Avoid arbitrary values when a design token or existing spacing/color scale fits.\\r\\n- Do not use color as the only way to communicate status.\\r\\n- Interactive elements must be keyboard accessible and expose accessible names and states.\\r\\n- Use buttons for actions and links for navigation.\\r\\n- Use correct heading hierarchy, labels, landmarks, and table semantics.\\r\\n- Dialogs and popovers must manage focus and support keyboard dismissal according to their interaction pattern.\\r\\n- Do not remove focus indicators without providing an accessible replacement.\\r\\n- Prefer accessible Testing Library queries (`getByRole`, `getByLabelText`) over CSS selectors.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 10. React Flow and workflow designer\\r\\n\\r\\n- Keep custom node components separate from workflow execution/business rules.\\r\\n- Keep node rendering, node configuration forms, and node metadata distinct where this improves cohesion.\\r\\n- Use stable node and edge identifiers.\\r\\n- Avoid mutating React Flow node/edge objects directly; use the supported state update APIs.\\r\\n- Keep canvas-only transient state in the designer store, not in persisted workflow definitions unless it is part of the domain contract.\\r\\n- Validate workflow structure on the backend as well as in the UI.\\r\\n- Handle large canvases carefully; avoid unnecessary updates to every node when one node changes.\\r\\n- Document any custom connection, selection, or keyboard interaction that differs from React Flow defaults.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 11. Error, loading, and empty states\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery asynchronous user-facing view must intentionally handle the states relevant to its operation:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Initial loading\\r\\n- Empty result\\r\\n- Success\\r\\n- Recoverable error\\r\\n- Permission/authorization failure\\r\\n- Pending mutation or submission\\r\\n\\r\\nRules:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Show actionable, understandable feedback; do not expose stack traces or internal server details.\\r\\n- Preserve useful context when an action fails.\\r\\n- Use the shared notification/error presentation where available.\\r\\n- Do not show duplicate notifications for the same error at multiple layers.\\r\\n- A `catch` block must handle, enrich, or rethrow the error. Never leave it empty.\\r\\n- Cancellation and expected control-flow outcomes should not be reported as unexpected errors.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 12. Async behavior and real-time updates\\r\\n\\r\\n- Handle loading, cancellation, retries, and component unmounting deliberately.\\r\\n- Avoid fire-and-forget promises. Await them, return them, or explicitly handle rejection.\\r\\n- For SSE or WebSocket clients, define connection lifecycle, reconnect policy, event validation, deduplication, and terminal-state behavior.\\r\\n- Clean up subscriptions and listeners when their owner unmounts.\\r\\n- Do not assume events arrive exactly once or in order unless the server contract guarantees it.\\r\\n- Keep polling as a deliberate fallback with bounded frequency and a clear stop condition.\\r\\n- Do not log credentials, tokens, personal data, or full sensitive payloads.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 13. Performance\\r\\n\\r\\n- Measure before optimizing.\\r\\n- Avoid unnecessary global state subscriptions and broad query invalidations.\\r\\n- Paginate or virtualize genuinely large collections.\\r\\n- Debounce user input only when it improves the interaction and define when the pending value is committed.\\r\\n- Lazy-load large route-level or editor-only dependencies where beneficial.\\r\\n- Keep expensive parsing and transformations out of repeated render paths when profiling shows a problem.\\r\\n- Do not sacrifice correctness or accessibility for speculative performance gains.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 14. Constants and configuration\\r\\n\\r\\n- Replace unexplained values with named constants when they represent policy, domain meaning, or reused configuration.\\r\\n- Keep environment-specific values in environment configuration, not source code.\\r\\n- Validate required environment variables at application startup.\\r\\n- Never commit secrets, API keys, tokens, or production credentials.\\r\\n- Avoid creating enums for every two-value local condition; use literal unions when simpler.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 15. Logging and debugging\\r\\n\\r\\n- Remove temporary `console.log`, debugger statements, and test-only code before merging.\\r\\n- Use the project's approved logging/telemetry mechanism for production diagnostics.\\r\\n- Do not log secrets, access tokens, credential values, or sensitive user payloads.\\r\\n- Include useful identifiers (for example, workflow ID or execution ID) when they help trace an operation and are safe to expose.\\r\\n- Avoid logging the same error at every layer.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 16. Comments and documentation\\r\\n\\r\\n- Prefer expressive names and simple control flow over comments that repeat the code.\\r\\n- Comment the reason, invariant, trade-off, or non-obvious behavior—not what the next line does.\\r\\n- Document public feature contracts and complex state transitions.\\r\\n- Mark temporary work with an owner or issue reference where possible; avoid permanent vague TODOs.\\r\\n- Update relevant documentation when changing behavior, API contracts, environment variables, or developer workflows.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 17. Testing\\r\\n\\r\\n### 17.1 Test location and naming\\r\\n\\r\\nKeep tests close to the code they verify unless the repository's test tooling or integration setup requires a centralized test directory.\\r\\n\\r\\n| Subject | Example |\\r\\n|---|---|\\r\\n| Component | `WorkflowCard.test.tsx` |\\r\\n| Hook | `useWorkflowMutations.test.tsx` |\\r\\n| Service | `workflow.service.test.ts` |\\r\\n| Store | `designer.store.test.ts` |\\r\\n| Utility | `parse-cron.test.ts` |\\r\\n\\r\\nFollow the repository's actual Vitest configuration. Do not exclude tests from type-checking without an explicit reason.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 17.2 What to test\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Utilities and transformations:** normal cases, boundary values, invalid inputs.\\r\\n- **Stores:** initial state, actions, reset behavior, and important invariants.\\r\\n- **Services/API modules:** request construction, mapping, and error propagation.\\r\\n- **Hooks:** query keys, mutation lifecycle, cache invalidation, and error behavior.\\r\\n- **Components:** user-visible behavior, validation, loading/error/empty states, accessibility, and important interactions.\\r\\n- **Workflow designer:** node/edge updates, selection, configuration, and persistence boundaries.\\r\\n- **Real-time clients:** reconnect behavior, duplicate events, invalid payloads, cleanup, and terminal states.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 17.3 Test practices\\r\\n\\r\\n- Test observable behavior rather than implementation details.\\r\\n- Prefer role- and label-based queries.\\r\\n- Use `userEvent` for realistic interactions.\\r\\n- Mock external boundaries (HTTP, browser APIs, third-party services), not every internal function.\\r\\n- Use MSW or the project's established API mocking approach when it provides more realistic request-level tests.\\r\\n- Avoid brittle snapshots for complex UI.\\r\\n- Keep tests deterministic; control time, randomness, and asynchronous events where necessary.\\r\\n- Do not assert only that a mocked function was called when a user-visible outcome can be tested.\\r\\n- Add regression tests for bugs before or alongside the fix.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 17.4 Coverage\\r\\n\\r\\nCoverage is a diagnostic, not a substitute for meaningful assertions. Prioritize critical workflow paths, authorization-sensitive UI, data transformations, and complex state transitions. Agree on thresholds in CI and raise them incrementally; do not add meaningless tests just to satisfy a percentage.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 18. Security and privacy\\r\\n\\r\\n- Treat all user input, URL parameters, API responses, and external connector data as untrusted.\\r\\n- Do not store secrets or access tokens in source code or browser storage unless the authentication design explicitly requires it and the risks are addressed.\\r\\n- Do not render untrusted HTML. If rich HTML is required, sanitize it with an approved sanitizer.\\r\\n- Avoid leaking sensitive data in URLs, logs, error messages, analytics, or client-side state.\\r\\n- Enforce authorization on the backend; frontend route guards are user experience, not a security boundary.\\r\\n- Validate file type, size, and user permissions before upload.\\r\\n- Do not weaken Content Security Policy or browser security settings to work around a development issue.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 19. Imports and module exports\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the configured `@/` alias for imports from the frontend source root.\\r\\n\\r\\nRecommended grouping:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. React and framework imports\\r\\n2. Third-party packages\\r\\n3. Application aliases\\r\\n4. Relative imports\\r\\n5. Styles\\r\\n\\r\\nFollow the formatter and lint rules for ordering. Avoid barrel files that create cycles or hide expensive imports. A feature `index.ts` should export only its intended public API.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 20. Pull request checklist\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore requesting review:\\r\\n\\r\\n- [ ] The change follows feature and module boundaries.\\r\\n- [ ] No new `any`, unsafe cast, or unexplained non-null assertion was introduced.\\r\\n- [ ] Server state and client state have the correct owners.\\r\\n- [ ] Loading, empty, error, and success states are handled where applicable.\\r\\n- [ ] Keyboard and screen-reader behavior is considered for interactive UI.\\r\\n- [ ] Tests cover the behavior and important regression cases.\\r\\n- [ ] No debug logs, secrets, or sensitive payloads are included.\\r\\n- [ ] Relevant documentation and API contracts are updated.\\r\\n- [ ] Frontend lint, type-check, tests, and build pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 21. Exceptions\\r\\n\\r\\nA rule may be intentionally broken when there is a concrete technical reason. The author must:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Explain why the standard approach is unsuitable.\\r\\n2. Describe the scope and risk of the exception.\\r\\n3. Add a comment or ADR when the exception affects architecture or future maintenance.\\r\\n4. Add a follow-up issue if the exception is temporary.\\r\\n\\r\\nDo not add broad lint or TypeScript suppressions to bypass a local problem.\\r\\nspecs\\\\workflow-storing-drafts\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\execution-item-model\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\workflow-restore-version\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\chatbot-nodes\\\\spec.md\\n.agents/scripts\\\\validate_component_spec.py\\nspecs\\\\workflow-publish-validation\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\chat-trigger\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\node-ux-and-inspector\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\workflow-platform-foundation\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\backend-integration-packages-modularization\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\workflow-draft-persistence\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\mvp-execution\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\backend-execution-engine-refactoring\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\workflow-designer-mvp\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\workflow-design\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\backend-cqrs-and-clean-architecture-alignment\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\authentication\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\real-time-execution-monitoring\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\admin-management\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\frontend-data-layer-standardization\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\move-manual-trigger-to-asynchonous\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\admin-management\\\\7.audit-log-viewer\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\frontend-component-decomposition\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\llm-node\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\admin-management\\\\6.execution-monitoring\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\execution-module-foundation\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\admin-management\\\\5.node-type-management\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\admin-management\\\\3.dashboard\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\admin-management\\\\4.connector-management\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\admin-management\\\\2.user-management\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\admin-management\\\\1.admin-foundation\\\\spec.md\\nspecs\\\\publish-workflow-version\\\\spec.md\\n[\\r\\n This is powerful for\r\nenforcing safety guards, running linters, auto-formatting code, or capturing\r\ncustom diagnostics.\r\n\r\nHooks are configured in a single `hooks.json` file placed in your customization\r\nroot directory (e.g., `.agents/hooks.json`).\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## File Format\r\n\r\nThe `hooks.json` file is a JSON object where each top-level key is a **hook\r\nname**, mapping to its event configuration.\r\n\r\n```json\r\n{\r\n  \"lint-checker\": {\r\n    \"PostToolUse\": [\r\n      {\r\n        \"matcher\": \"run_command\",\r\n        \"hooks\": [\r\n          {\r\n            \"type\": \"command\",\r\n            \"command\": \"./scripts/lint.sh\",\r\n            \"timeout\": 10\r\n          }\r\n        ]\r\n      }\r\n    ]\r\n  },\r\n  \"safety-gate\": {\r\n    \"enabled\": false,\r\n    \"PreToolUse\": [\r\n      {\r\n        \"matcher\": \"run_command\",\r\n        \"hooks\": [\r\n          {\r\n            \"command\": \"./scripts/safety-check.sh\"\r\n          }\r\n        ]\r\n      }\r\n    ]\r\n  },\r\n  \"reminder\": {\r\n    \"PreInvocation\": [\r\n      {\r\n        \"type\": \"command\",\r\n        \"command\": \"./scripts/reminder.sh\"\r\n      }\r\n    ]\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n*   **Merging**: Multiple named hooks (e.g., from different plugins or configs)\r\n    for the same event type are merged and executed sequentially.\r\n*   **Disabling**: Set `\"enabled\": false` at the hook level to temporarily\r\n    disable all its handlers.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Hook Spec Fields\r\n\r\nEach named hook supports:\r\n\r\n*   **`enabled`** (bool, optional): Defaults to `true`. Set to `false` to\r\n    disable.\r\n*   **`SessionStart`** (array, optional): Handlers running once when a new\r\n    conversation session starts.\r\n*   **`PreToolUse`** (array, optional): Handlers running before a tool executes.\r\n*   **`PostToolUse`** (array, optional): Handlers running after a tool\r\n    completes.\r\n*   **`PreInvocation`** (array, optional): Handlers running before the model is\r\n    called.\r\n*   **`PostInvocation`** (array, optional): Handlers running after tool calls\r\n    finish.\r\n*   **`Stop`** (array, optional): Handlers running when the execution loop\r\n    terminates.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Supported Event Types\r\n\r\n| Event            | When it fires     | Matcher target   | Structure         |\r\n| :--------------- | :---------------- | :--------------- | :---------------- |\r\n| `SessionStart`   | Once when a new   | N/A (ignored).   | Flat (list of     |\r\n:                  : session starts.   :                  : handler objects   :\r\n:                  :                   :                  : directly).        :\r\n| `PreToolUse`     | Before a tool     | Tool name (e.g., | Grouped (uses     |\r\n:                  : step executes.    : `run_command`).  : `matcher` &       :\r\n:                  :                   :                  : `hooks` wrapper). :\r\n| `PostToolUse`    | After a tool step | Tool name (e.g., | Grouped (uses     |\r\n:                  : completes.        : `run_command`).  : `matcher` &       :\r\n:                  :                   :                  : `hooks` wrapper). :\r\n| `PreInvocation`  | Before the model  | N/A (ignored).   | Flat (list of     |\r\n:                  : is called.        :                  : handler objects   :\r\n:                  :                   :                  : directly).        :\r\n| `PostInvocation` | After tool calls  | N/A (ignored).   | Flat (list of     |\r\n:                  : finish.           :                  : handler objects   :\r\n:                  :                   :                  : directly).        :\r\n| `Stop`           | When the          | N/A (ignored).   | Flat (list of     |\r\n:                  : execution loop    :                  : handler objects   :\r\n:                  : terminates.       :                  : directly).        :\r\n\r\n### The Matcher\r\n\r\nFor tool-specific events (`PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`), you must wrap the\r\nhandlers in a group with a `matcher` regex:\r\n\r\n*   `\"matcher\": \"*\"` or `\"\"`: Matches all tools.\r\n*   `\"matcher\": \"run_command\"`: Matches exactly `run_command`.\r\n*   `\"matcher\": \"run_command\\|view_file\"`: Matches either tool.\r\n*   `\"matcher\": \"browser_.*\"`: Matches any tool starting with `browser_`.\r\n\r\nTool names are derived by lowercasing the step type and removing the\r\n`CORTEX_STEP_TYPE_` prefix.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Hook Handler Fields\r\n\r\nEach individual hook handler object supports:\r\n\r\n*   **`type`** (string, optional): Defaults to `\"command\"`. Only `\"command\"`\r\n    (shell execution) is currently supported.\r\n*   **`command`** (string, required): The shell command to execute (run via `sh\r\n    -c` on Unix, `cmd /c` on Windows). `~` is expanded to the home directory.\r\n    The working directory is set to the directory containing `hooks.json`.\r\n*   **`timeout`** (int, optional): Execution timeout in seconds. Defaults to\r\n    `30`.\r\n\r\nCommand hooks that ship with a plugin also get `PLUGIN_ROOT` (the plugin\r\ndirectory) and, for installed plugins, `PLUGIN_DATA` (the plugin's persistent\r\ndata directory) as environment variables, the same paths the plugin's MCP\r\nservers get. They are not substituted into `command`: hooks run in the plugin\r\nfolder, so refer to bundled files with a relative path, and use the variables\r\nfor the rest, e.g. `\"command\": \"./hooks/check.sh --cache \\\"$PLUGIN_DATA\\\"\"`.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Input/Output Contract\r\n\r\nHook commands receive context as a JSON object on **stdin** and must output\r\ntheir result as a JSON object on **stdout**.\r\n\r\n> [!IMPORTANT] All JSON keys in the hook payloads use **camelCase** (protojson\r\n> encoding), e.g., `conversationId` and `stepIdx`.\r\n\r\n### Common Input Fields\r\n\r\nEvery hook payload sent to `stdin` includes these common system metadata fields:\r\n\r\n```json\r\n{\r\n  \"conversationId\": \"ec33ebf9-0cba-4100-8142-c61503f6c587\",\r\n  \"workspacePaths\": [\"/path/to/workspace\"],\r\n  \"transcriptPath\": \"/path/to/workspace/.gemini/antigravity/transcript.jsonl\",\r\n  \"artifactDirectoryPath\": \"/path/to/workspace/.gemini/antigravity/artifacts\",\r\n  \"modelName\": \"auto\",\r\n  \"lastUserInput\": \"Fix the failing unit test\",\r\n  \"hookEventName\": \"PreToolUse\",\r\n  \"cwd\": \"/path/to/workspace\"\r\n}\r\n```\r\n\r\n`hookEventName` is the event being run, as written in `hooks.json`. `cwd` is the\r\nagent's working directory, where its commands run by default; it is not the hook\r\nprocess's working directory.\r\n\r\n> [!NOTE]\r\n> The `transcriptPath` and `artifactDirectoryPath` are product-specific. The\r\n> example above uses `antigravity` (for Antigravity 2.0). Depending on the\r\n> interface you are using, the directory name will differ:\r\n> *   **CLI**: `antigravity-cli/`\r\n> *   **Antigravity 2.0**: `antigravity/`\r\n> *   **IDE**: `antigravity-ide/`\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 1. `PreToolUse` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to gate, block, or audit tool executions.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"toolCall\": {\r\n        \"id\": \"toolu_01\",\r\n        \"name\": \"run_command\",\r\n        \"args\": {\r\n          \"CommandLine\": \"npm test\"\r\n        }\r\n      },\r\n      \"stepIdx\": 19,\r\n      ... (common fields)\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"decision\": \"ask\",\r\n      \"reason\": \"Requires confirmation for test execution.\",\r\n      \"permissionOverrides\": [\"command(npm test)\"]\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`decision`** (string, required):\r\n        *   `\"allow\"`: Automatically allow the tool execution.\r\n        *   `\"deny\"`: Hard block the execution immediately.\r\n        *   `\"ask\"`: Prompt the user for permission (respects \"Always Allow\"\r\n            cache).\r\n        *   `\"force_ask\"`: Always prompt the user, ignoring cached permissions.\r\n    *   **`reason`** (string, optional): Explanation shown to the user/agent.\r\n    *   **`permissionOverrides`** (array of strings, optional): Temporary\r\n        permission grants.\r\n    *   **`overwrite`** (object, optional): Key-value pairs merged into the tool\r\n        call's arguments before it runs. This is a **shallow, top-level** merge:\r\n        each key replaces the value at that key in the tool call's arguments\r\n        (nested objects are replaced wholesale, not deep-merged). The modified\r\n        tool call is what actually executes and is recorded. Example:\r\n\r\n        ```json\r\n        {\r\n          \"decision\": \"allow\",\r\n          \"overwrite\": {\r\n            \"CommandLine\": \"ls -la\"\r\n          }\r\n        }\r\n        ```\r\n\r\n        The agent replays the tool call it originally emitted, so when\r\n        `overwrite` changes the arguments the tool result is prefixed with a\r\n        notice telling the agent that a hook rewrote them, naming the keys that\r\n        changed.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 2. `PostToolUse` Contract\r\n\r\nUse for post-execution cleanup, auto-fixes, or analysis.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"toolCall\": {\r\n        \"id\": \"toolu_01\", // Same id as in PreToolUse for this call\r\n        \"name\": \"run_command\",\r\n        \"args\": {\r\n          \"CommandLine\": \"npm test\"\r\n        }\r\n      },\r\n      \"stepIdx\": 5,\r\n      \"error\": \"exit status 1\", // Present if the tool failed\r\n      ... (common fields)\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**: Expects an empty JSON object `{}`.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 3. `SessionStart` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to inject welcome messages, initial instructions, or startup context once at\r\nthe beginning of a new conversation session.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**: Common system metadata fields (`conversationId`,\r\n    `workspacePaths`, `transcriptPath`, `artifactDirectoryPath`, `modelName`).\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"injectSteps\": [\r\n        {\r\n          \"systemMessage\": {\r\n            \"systemMessage\": \"Welcome! Custom workspace environment is active.\"\r\n          }\r\n        }\r\n      ]\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array of objects, optional): Steps to inject at\r\n        session startup. Accepts the same step types as `PreInvocation` (see\r\n        below).\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 4. `PreInvocation` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to gate user prompts or inject context or instructions before the model\r\nruns.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"invocationNum\": 3,\r\n      \"initialNumSteps\": 10,\r\n      ... (common fields)\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"injectSteps\": [\r\n        {\r\n          \"systemMessage\": {\r\n            \"systemMessage\": \"Remember to check for lint errors before proposing changes.\"\r\n          }\r\n        }\r\n      ],\r\n      \"terminationBehavior\": \"\"\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array of objects, optional): Steps to inject.\r\n        Supported step types:\r\n        *   `{\"systemMessage\": {\"systemMessage\": \"...\", \"metadata\": {...}}}`\r\n            (persists in the conversation trajectory across turns; note that\r\n            `systemMessage` requires a nested object rather than a plain string).\r\n            To show markdown to the user that the model never reads, set\r\n            `metadata.user_markdown`: the UI renders it inline instead of a\r\n            collapsible \"System Message\" row, and the model sees only the\r\n            `systemMessage` text (leave it `\"\"` to send the model nothing).\r\n        *   `{\"userMessage\": \"...\"}` (plain string)\r\n        *   `{\"ephemeralMessage\": \"...\"}` *(deprecated — use `systemMessage`\r\n            instead; converted to a hidden system message)*\r\n        *   `{\"errorMessage\": {\"userMessage\": \"...\"}}` (shows an error message\r\n            to the user without sending it to the model. Marks the turn as\r\n            errored with a non-zero exit code in non-interactive `-p` mode, and\r\n            does not stop execution on its own unless paired with\r\n            `\"terminationBehavior\": \"terminate\"`).\r\n    *   **`terminationBehavior`** (string, optional):\r\n        *   `\"terminate\"`: Stops execution immediately before calling the model\r\n            (pair with an injected `errorMessage` to show a block message to the\r\n            user).\r\n        *   `\"\"` (or omitted): Proceeds to the model call (default).\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 5. `PostInvocation` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to inspect model outputs and potentially force continuation.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**: Same as `PreInvocation` input.\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"injectSteps\": [],\r\n      \"terminationBehavior\": \"force_continue\"\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`injectSteps`** (array, optional): Steps to inject (same supported\r\n        step types as `PreInvocation`).\r\n    *   **`terminationBehavior`** (string, optional):\r\n        *   `\"force_continue\"`: Forces the execution loop to continue.\r\n        *   `\"terminate\"`: Forces the loop to stop (pair with an injected\r\n            `errorMessage` to show a block message to the user).\r\n        *   `\"\"` (or omitted): Default behavior.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n### 6. `Stop` Contract\r\n\r\nUse to prevent the agent from stopping if goals are not met.\r\n\r\n*   **Input (stdin)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"executionNum\": 1,\r\n      \"terminationReason\": \"NO_TOOL_CALL\", // e.g., \"NO_TOOL_CALL\", \"MAX_INVOCATIONS\", \"ERROR\", \"USER_CANCELED\", \"TERMINAL_STEP_TYPE\"\r\n      \"error\": \"\", // Present if stopped due to error\r\n      \"fullyIdle\": true, // true if all background tasks are done\r\n      \"continuationActive\": true, // true after a Stop hook kept the agent going this turn (executionNum > 0)\r\n      ... (common fields)\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n    *   **`continuationActive`** (boolean): `true` while the agent is doing\r\n        work that a `Stop` hook asked for in this turn; `false` again once a\r\n        new user message starts a turn. Check it so a hook that asks the agent\r\n        to continue doesn't ask again in a loop.\r\n    *   **`terminationReason`** (string): The `ExecutorTerminationReason` enum\r\n        name with the `EXECUTOR_TERMINATION_REASON_` prefix stripped:\r\n        *   `\"NO_TOOL_CALL\"`: Normal turn completion (model finished without\r\n            calling a tool).\r\n        *   `\"MAX_INVOCATIONS\"`: Hit the maximum invocation limit.\r\n        *   `\"MAX_FORCED_INVOCATIONS\"`: Hit the forced-continuation limit.\r\n        *   `\"MAX_TOKEN_BUDGET_EXCEEDED\"`: Exceeded the token budget.\r\n        *   `\"ERROR\"`: Execution terminated due to an error.\r\n        *   `\"USER_CANCELED\"`: Canceled by the user.\r\n        *   `\"TERMINAL_STEP_TYPE\"`: Stopped on a terminal step type.\r\n        *   `\"TERMINAL_CUSTOM_HOOK\"`: Terminated by a custom `PreInvocation` or\r\n            `PostInvocation` hook.\r\n        *   `\"INJECTED_RESPONSE\"`: Terminated after an injected response.\r\n\r\n*   **Output (stdout)**:\r\n\r\n    ```json\r\n    {\r\n      \"decision\": \"continue\",\r\n      \"reason\": \"The tests are still running in the background. Please wait.\"\r\n    }\r\n    ```\r\n\r\n    *   **`decision`** (string, required): Set to `\"continue\"` to block the stop\r\n        and re-enter the loop. Any other value allows the agent to stop.\r\n    *   **`reason`** (string, optional): Injected as a system message if\r\n        continuing.\r\n\r\n--------------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n\r\n## Current Limitations\r\n\r\n*   Only `type: \"command\"` is supported (no HTTP or prompt hooks yet).\r\n*   Hooks run synchronously and block the agent loop (no async execution).\r\n"}}
{"index":2,"status":"fulfilled","value":{"chunk_id":"2daf46","wall_time_seconds":0.6756145,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":4020,"output":"- Use fake or in-memory repositories for application tests when they provide meaningful behavior and are simpler than mocking every repository method.\r\n- Mock external boundaries when testing failure handling, rare responses, or provider interactions.\r\n- Use real disposable PostgreSQL/Redis services for integration tests that claim to verify those integrations.\r\n- Do not mock Prisma internals in tests intended to validate Prisma repository behavior.\r\n- Do not require every dependency to be mocked in every test category.\r\n\r\n### 16.3 What to test\r\n\r\nPrioritize:\r\n- Domain invariants and state transitions.\r\n- Workflow draft, publish, and immutable-version behavior.\r\n- Execution lifecycle, idempotency, retries, cancellation, and terminal-state protection.\r\n- Queue publishing/consumption and failure recovery.\r\n- Connector credential lifecycle, OAuth callback validation, timeouts, and unsafe URL rejection.\r\n- Authentication, authorization, tenant/resource ownership, and admin actions.\r\n- API validation and response contracts.\r\n- Persistence constraints and transaction behavior.\r\n\r\n### 16.4 Test quality\r\n\r\n- Test outcomes and invariants, not private implementation details.\r\n- Include success, failure, boundary, and concurrency cases where relevant.\r\n- Keep tests deterministic. Control clocks, randomness, and external responses.\r\n- Use descriptive test names that state the expected behavior.\r\n- Add regression tests for fixed defects.\r\n- Avoid tests that pass only because all dependencies are mocked to return the expected answer.\r\n- Coverage is a signal, not proof of correctness. Prioritize critical paths and meaningful assertions.\r\n\r\n## 17. Git and pull request conventions\r\n\r\nUse Conventional Commits:\r\n\r\n```text\r\nfeat(execution): add node retry policy\r\nfix(connector): reject private network targets\r\nrefactor(workflow): simplify version mapping\r\ntest(execution): cover duplicate job delivery\r\ndocs(architecture): document module boundaries\r\nchore(ci): run backend type-check\r\n```\r\n\r\nPull requests should:\r\n\r\n- Address one coherent concern.\r\n- Explain intent and behavior changes.\r\n- Include tests and relevant migration/configuration notes.\r\n- Call out security, data, or operational impact.\r\n- Include screenshots or recordings for meaningful UI/API consumer changes where useful.\r\n- Avoid mixing broad formatting changes with behavioral refactoring.\r\n\r\n## 18. Pull request checklist\r\n\r\n- [ ] Dependencies follow the intended architecture direction.\r\n- [ ] No domain/application code imports Nest HTTP exceptions or infrastructure implementations.\r\n- [ ] Cross-module access uses an intentional public contract.\r\n- [ ] No new `any`, unsafe cast, floating promise, or broad lint suppression.\r\n- [ ] Errors are translated at the correct boundary.\r\n- [ ] Input, authorization, and tenant/resource ownership are validated.\r\n- [ ] Database changes include migrations and appropriate constraints.\r\n- [ ] Jobs and event handlers account for duplicate delivery and retry behavior.\r\n- [ ] Logs contain useful correlation context but no secrets or sensitive payloads.\r\n- [ ] Tests cover success, failure, and relevant edge cases.\r\n- [ ] Backend lint, type-check, tests, and build pass.\r\n- [ ] Relevant docs, environment examples, and API contracts are updated.\r\n\r\n## 19. Exceptions\r\n\r\nAn exception is acceptable only when the standard approach is unsuitable for a concrete reason. Document:\r\n\r\n1. The rule being bypassed.\r\n2. Why the exception is necessary.\r\n3. Its scope and risk.\r\n4. Whether it is permanent or temporary.\r\n5. A follow-up issue when remediation is expected.\r\n\r\nPrefer a narrow exception over weakening a project-wide rule.\r\n#!/usr/bin/env node\r\n/**\r\n * Did the agent follow the rules? Read what the hooks recorded and say so.\r\n *\r\n * The hooks write one event per session start, per UI edit (with what each\r\n * fast gate found) and per end of turn (which pages were measured, and whether\r\n * the Stop hook passed, blocked, or let the agent go while pages were still\r\n * open). Gate receipts say what was measured and when. This joins the two into\r\n * one verdict per session, from rules that can be counted:\r\n *\r\n *   1. measured   every page edited had a passing gate before the session ended\r\n *   2. fixed      no fast-gate finding was left on a file's latest version\r\n *   3. not quit   the agent never finished while the Stop hook still had pages open\r\n *\r\n * It reports counts, never a score for taste, and reads local files only.\r\n *\r\n * Usage:\r\n *   node scripts/observe.mjs                  sessions from the last 7 days, every project\r\n *   node scripts/observe.mjs --project [dir]  only this project (default: cwd)\r\n *   node scripts/observe.mjs --days 30\r\n *   node scripts/observe.mjs --session <id>   one session's timeline (a prefix is enough)\r\n *   node scripts/observe.mjs --last           the most recent session's timeline\r\n *   node scripts/observe.mjs --json           the same data, machine-readable\r\n *   node scripts/observe.mjs --html [file]    write a dashboard page (default: observe.html)\r\n *   node scripts/observe.mjs --data <dir>     read this data dir instead of searching\r\n */\r\nimport { readFileSync, readdirSync, existsSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';\r\nimport { join, resolve, relative, basename, extname } from 'node:path';\r\nimport { homedir } from 'node:os';\r\nimport { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';\r\n\r\nconst ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('..', import.meta.url));\r\nconst PAGES = new Set(['.html', '.htm']);\r\n/** A gate run counts toward a session if it happened up to this long after the last event. */\r\nconst GRACE_MS = 15 * 60 * 1000;\r\n\r\nfunction readJsonl(f) {\r\n  try {\r\n    return readFileSync(f, 'utf8').split('\\n').filter(Boolean)\r\n      .map(l => { try { return JSON.parse(l); } catch { return null; } }).filter(Boolean);\r\n  } catch { return []; }\r\n}\r\nconst list = d => { try { return readdirSync(d); } catch { return []; } };\r\n\r\n/** Every data dir the hooks may have written to on this machine. */\r\nexport function dataDirs({ data, cwd = process.cwd() } = {}) {\r\n  if (data) return [resolve(data)];\r\n  const dirs = [];\r\n  if (process.env.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA) dirs.push(process.env.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA);\r\n  const plugins = join(homedir(), '.claude/plugins/data');\r\n  for (const d of list(plugins)) if (d.startsWith('ux-ui-agent-skills')) dirs.push(join(plugins, d));\r\n  // A clone or `init` install keeps everything in the project.\r\n  dirs.push(join(cwd, '.ds-receipts'));\r\n  return [...new Set(dirs.map(d => resolve(d)))].filter(d => existsSync(d));\r\n}\r\n\r\n/** Events live in <data>/observe, or in <project>/.ds-receipts/observe. */\r\nexport function loadEvents(dirs) {\r\n  const out = [];\r\n  for (const d of dirs) {\r\n    const ev = join(d, 'observe');\r\n    for (const f of list(ev)) if (/^events-.*\\.jsonl$/.test(f)) out.push(...readJsonl(join(ev, f)));\r\n  }\r\n  return out.filter(e => e && e.v === 1 && e.session && e.at).sort((a, b) => a.at.localeCompare(b.at));\r\n}\r\n\r\n/** Receipts from the data dirs and from every project the events name. */\r\nexport function loadReceipts(dirs, projects = []) {\r\n  const places = new Set();\r\n  for (const d of dirs) places.add(basename(d) === '.ds-receipts' ? d : join(d, 'receipts'));\r\n#!/usr/bin/env node\r\n/**\r\n * Run a gate and leave a receipt.\r\n *\r\n * \"Never state a number you did not measure\" is the first rule in this repo and\r\n * the only one with nothing behind it: a model can type a contrast ratio as\r\n * easily as it can measure one, and nothing downstream can tell the difference.\r\n *\r\n * This wrapper closes that. It runs the gate with DS_REQUIRE_BROWSER=1 always\r\n * set, so a missing browser fails instead of printing SKIPPED and exiting 0, and\r\n * writes one JSON line per run: which gate, which file, the SHA-256 of that file\r\n * AT THE MOMENT IT WAS MEASURED, the exit code, the numbers the gate printed, and\r\n * when.\r\n *\r\n * The hash is the point. A receipt is only evidence while the file still hashes\r\n * the same; edit the file and the receipt is expired, which is exactly the case\r\n * where a remembered number becomes a lie.\r\n *\r\n * Usage:\r\n *   node scripts/gate.mjs verify_states.mjs examples/sample-app/preview.html\r\n *   node scripts/gate.mjs --list                 # receipts for this session\r\n *   node scripts/gate.mjs --check <file>         # is there a live receipt for it?\r\n *   node scripts/gate.mjs --dir                  # print the receipts directory\r\n *\r\n * Exit code is the gate's own. --check exits 1 when a file has no live receipt.\r\n */\r\nimport { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';\r\nimport { createHash } from 'node:crypto';\r\nimport { mkdirSync, appendFileSync, readFileSync, existsSync, readdirSync } from 'node:fs';\r\nimport { join, resolve, relative, extname, dirname } from 'node:path';\r\nimport { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';\r\n\r\nconst ROOT = fileURLToPath(new URL('..', import.meta.url));\r\n\r\n/** Where receipts live. A plugin gets a data dir; a clone keeps them in-tree. */\r\nexport function receiptsDir() {\r\n  const data = process.env.CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA;\r\n  if (data) return join(data, 'receipts');\r\n  return join(process.env.CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR || process.cwd(), '.ds-receipts');\r\n}\r\n\r\n/** One file per session, so a receipt from a previous session is never reused. */\r\nfunction receiptsFile() {\r\n  const id = process.env.CLAUDE_SESSION_ID || 'local';\r\n  return join(receiptsDir(), `${id.replace(/[^\\w-]/g, '')}.jsonl`);\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function hashFile(p) {\r\n  try {\r\n    return createHash('sha256').update(readFileSync(p)).digest('hex').slice(0, 16);\r\n  } catch {\r\n    return null;\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\n/**\r\n * The numbers a gate printed. Deliberately shallow: this records what was on\r\n * screen so a later claim can be checked against it, and does not try to parse\r\n * every gate's format into a schema that would rot.\r\n
